The same could be done for the selfemployed, where you could say the best income year in the last five years. It would be a relief for the self-employed, it would be a simplification for the cashiers, and that would be a relief for the employees who have been in trouble because of this. It would give both employees and self-employed a longer period of time to be calculated and provide some options within it. So, of course, all income in this 36-month period is supposed to be included in the recalculation, even if, for example, in the intervening period. has had a low-paid job as coronater or something else. So the conditions which are a problem for the self-employed are also a problem for the individual employee. That is why we think that there are quite a lot of very inappropriate ways in which this law has been formulated, and it is our proposal that the Ministry itself should submit some amendments. I have given some key words to the easy solution, and that would be to extend the calculation period, and that would be to extend the reference period for the self-employed. Take a look at that. The draft law will allow independent operators to choose to calculate a rate of benefit where the financial year 2020 is missed and where the period for calculating the rate will be extended accordingly backwards in time. ParlaMint-DK_20210504165119	Thank you, I would like to start by saying, on top of one, I think, really good debate in this Chamber, and I would really like to thank you for a very constructive approach from the parties in looking at the legislative proposal here, which is intended to create a new framework for how we look after nature in Denmark. I would also like to thank all the citizens, organisations and stakeholders who cannot be seen in this Chamber but who have sent in proposals for consultation. It's nice to feel, I think, the great support there is that we are now taking this historic step for nature in Denmark. However, it is also clear that there are some points of focus, there are some questions and there are also some concerns to which we have responded in the consultation note, which have also been raised here today and in which we are going to delve into more. We're in the middle of a natural crisis. Animals and plants lack space and habitats, and there are also many animal and plant species, even at home in Denmark, which are completely in danger of extinction, and if we are going to reverse this development, and of course we are going to have to have several large, coherent natural areas. We must give nature space to be nature on its own terms, and that is a change in nature policy. We believe that this is the right way to go if we are to give nature the boost that is needed after for many years it has gone the other way. And that's exactly why we set aside this 888 million. kr. in the Finance Act, and thank you to the parties that are part of it. That is why we are now going to set up a total of 15 national natural parks. With the bill in this Chamber today, we are supposed to legalise the concept of the national park in Danish law. I think it's pretty big myself. At the same time, we will also set the framework for establishing and managing natural parks in state-owned areas in Denmark. With these new national parks, we will have the opportunity to establish more coherent areas where the main concern is to strengthen nature and biodiversity. We recreate the conditions for natural processes to unfold. The trees in the forest must be allowed to overturn, the water must run more freely, and we must keep the areas free of forest and agricultural production. Management shall also, as far as possible, not target one specific species or individual habitat type. Instead, focus should be on the natural processes that characterise the ecosystems to which the species are adapted. In this way, more natural ecosystems and also better habitats and living conditions can be created for plants, for mushrooms and for animals. One of the things that has then also been filled in today's debate is that we want to expose large herbivores in the areas. The big animals are important to nature. They are, among other things. because their grazing and movement help create light-open areas and create variety in nature. It gives greater diversity and more habitats, and thus the animals help nature along the way. The starting point for the large herbivores is that they have to live as wild as possible, and therefore it is also crucial that the designated areas are large enough and varied enough to support the needs of the animals all year round. However, when we put animals into enclosures, we also have a duty to ensure that the animals are well and that they thrive, and it is important for me to stress that we will not compromise animal welfare with the new national parks. So I hope it can also be a little reassuring in relation to the concern I have heard in the Chamber today, because I would very much like us to be a broad majority on the legislative framework. When we want more wild nature, it must not be at the expense of the animals we put into the national parks. It's not open for discussion. We must ensure that animals can find sufficient food, that they have the opportunity to shelter and shelter and that we are supervised so that we can react if animals become ill or show signs of food shortages. When we put out large animals, it also means that fences will have to be set up around the national parks, and that is also some of the stuff that has filled, including in the consultation answers. The fence must keep the animals exposed inside the national park; it must not keep people out of the national park, on the contrary. I really have great expectations that the new national parks will create some more exciting, unique and new natural experiences for the Danes. The Ministry of the Environment, together with the Danish Nature Conservation Association and the Free Air Council yesterday, has had a number of principles for nature and outdoor life in the national parks. These are principles of communication, about focus on children and young people, about recreational infrastructure, about consideration of vulnerable nature, about local involvement and about ongoing experience gathering, and I am incredibly happy that we can stand together to do something needed and good for nature and at the same time invite even more Danes into nature. So I also think that we are clearly saying that nature and open-air life are not each other's opposites, on the contrary. In the national parks, a great deal will be done to disseminate knowledge and information about nature and how best to travel in the areas, and that there will be room for new paths and lookout towers to experience nature. We need to find solutions that take into account both nature and outdoor life and that make it safe and secure to travel in the areas. One of the major questions is, of course, where the new national parks will lie. There are many opinions about this, and we are also interested in hearing them all. It must be an inclusive process. We have already made some progress with the first two that are on the way, and the next three that have been pointed out, and now the government joins with the circle of agreements for the nature and biodiversity package to invite to a conference before the summer, where we will invite widely for dialogue on the exciting perspectives in relation to natural parks across Denmark. We invite you to input, to suggestions and to ideas from citizens, municipalities, experts and stakeholders in relation to the locations of the remaining national parks. We look forward to this, and I hope that the national parks will be excited, even in addition to the Socialist rapporteur, and that we will then be able to see in the rings in the water out there. I think we already can. As I said, I hope that together we can find the right solutions and also ensure the local support that is important in this work. With the legislative framework we are putting forward here, I believe that we are delivering on an election promise that we gave before the elections, both to the Danes, but also to Denmark's nature. We provide the opportunity to free nature more for the benefit of plants, for animals, for biodiversity and for all of us who care so much about Denmark's nature. With that I would say that I look forward to the continued examination of the bill. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20210504174943	Thank you. There are no more brief remarks and no more rapporteurs except the rapporteur for the authors. Here you go, Mrs. Susanne Zimmer.
ParlaMint-DK_20210504175007	This proposal about old trees is that we will require the government to make a legally binding designation that protects and preserves old trees on public land. In fact, we would also have liked to have included private land, but we start with public land. This must be done in close cooperation with biodiversity researchers and stakeholders. Biodiversity is declining. Globally, we are in the middle of the sixth mass extinction. Scientists tell us that within the next few years we are in a situation where one million animal species and plant species will be eradicated globally. In Denmark it is approx. 40% of the Danish species threatened and listed in the IUCN inventory of red-listed species. Animals and plants are endangered because their habitats disappear. This is due to forestry, agriculture, urban development and various construction projects. We propose an increase in the protection and conservation of old trees, among others. because of the role of trees in maintaining biodiversity. Trees are small ecosystems. Veteran trees in a natural area can be considered as key biotopes, a home for many different species: moss, mushrooms, insects, birds and animals. In the forest, the preservation of old trees and their natural and undisturbed surroundings can play an important role as the home of a variety of species, in maintaining biodiversity and in ensuring natural hydrology around the tree. Trees bind CO 2 2 But trees also have a glory value, both in nature and in cities. They give the area an identity. We can see an alley in front of us and see how it is in relation to an area where there are no trees. They are carriers of our common cultural history. They carry the past, they carry our grandparents' history with them, and in the Free Greens we want to pass that story on to the next generations. We all experience fascination by seeing an old tree that has an impressive crown, or that grows incredibly crooked and has branches that hang far out where it is incomprehensible that it can carry their weight. The fascination of trees is not just something we experience as ordinary people; it is also something we see in our art, from golden age art to modern art. There are groups now working with forest bathing. We have heard of a rapporteur who hugs trees before, but there are also many who lie under the tree trunks and look up between the leaves and towards the sky and find peace there. In addition, large trees in the cities are essential for us to find shaded places in the summer, and also help lower the temperature in the cities, where it can gradually become warm in the summer. At the Natural History Museum in Gram, they had a parking lot to expand. An old oak tree stood in the way. It was 200 years old, and it could have been 1,000 years. Many solutions could have been found. The easiest was to have built around the tree, but it was cut down and so it is a natural history museum. This clearly shows the need for stricter legislation. Old trees and their history and value mean a lot to many people. An example of this is a group for tree enthusiasts on Facebook, which has also been mentioned earlier, and which has grown from 1,000 members to 5,000 members within a few months, and even Lea Wermelin has now joined the group. Many ordinary citizens have seen the values of the old trees. It also appears in the great interest of the life tree project run by the Danish Nature Agency and the designation of eternity trees run by the Danish Nature Conservation Association and where ordinary people designate trees they believe should be preserved. However, none of the projects are legally binding. The Greens' proposal is basically that we should give nature more space and be allowed to unfold on its own terms. That is why we also need to value nature differently and give it greater protection in our legislation. We need to cherish the old trees more. If we want to reverse the gloomy development that we are currently seeing moving forward, we must do so. Last week there was a debate in this Chamber about a sharper punishment for fauna crime. This means that if you lay poison to birds of prey or shoot a wolf, it must trigger a higher punishment. What if we could also find support to protect as much fauna as we do in the form of trees? To cut down trees should also be fauna delinquency. If you cut down a 200-year-old tree, you wipe out 200 years of history and biodiversity, and it takes 200 years to restore it. This is not an ambitious proposal, but it is important to protect old trees today. There are many places we can find inspiration for legislation in this area, for example. at Frederiksberg Municipality, at the City of Copenhagen and in England, where the rules have been tightened to preserve the trees and where there is a ban on the felling of trees of a certain age, of a certain size and with a certain cultural value. This has been done in the interests of the trees and the value of the trees for humans and for nature. Current legislation does not protect wholeheartedly old trees. We therefore propose a legally binding designation which protects and preserves old trees and hedgerows on public land. It is a designation that must be carried out in close cooperation with the green organisations and biodiversity researchers, so that we can have legislation that best reflects both the glory value and biodiversity value of trees for the benefit of both humans and nature. I would also refer to the comments accompanying the proposal and recommend it to the kind treatment of the matter.
ParlaMint-DK_20210504175641	Thank you. There are no brief comments to Mrs Susanne Zimmer. As no one else has asked to speak, the debate is closed. I propose that the proposal be referred to the Committee on the Environment and Food. Are there any comments? It's agreed.
ParlaMint-DK_20210504175716	The debate is open. The first to speak is the Minister for the Environment. Here you go.
ParlaMint-DK_20210504175720	Thank you. I almost want to look at Thorvald Stauning, who was one of Denmark's first environmentalists, and who, back in 1937, helped to secure the Danish beaches and coasts as free and accessible without construction. I won't spend more time on it, but it's nice to celebrate that we in Denmark have taken such good care of our coasts. And it also has a parallel to the proposal that is before us today. What the proposers are proposing is that the municipalities should be given the competence to grant permission for facilities on and in the case of bathing and boatbridges which are directly related to the objectives of the bathing and boatbridges. It is the State which is responsible for the overall interests and balances of interests in the coastal zone. This is the case on land, where beach protection and dune conservation protects the Danish coasts, and that is the case on the water where the state exercises the sovereignty over the Danish territorial sea. This is a fundamental principle, and the government wants to maintain that principle. The state's exercise of the sovereignty over the territorial sea must support both recreational development opportunities and, at the same time, safeguard the open coasts, as I began by saying. On land there is generally a ban on building, construction and facilities on the beach and coastal areas behind the beach. It follows from the provisions of the Nature Conservation Act on beach protection and dune conservation. It is important that there should be no circumvention of the principle of freeing the coastal zone from installations. Benches, terraces etc., which cannot obtain permission on land, should of course not instead be moved onto the water. The location of installations on the territorial sea requires the authorisation of the State in accordance with the rules of the Coastal Protection Act. An exception to that is smaller bathing and boat bridges. The authority to grant authorization was delegated to the counties, which were then regional conservation authorities, by decree on bathing and boatbridges in 1981. At the time of the municipal reform in 2007, the municipalities then took over the competence of the counties. The smaller bridges which may be authorised by the municipalities must have a simple and simple structure without unnecessary fittings and must therefore not, as a rule, be provided with furniture or other foreign elements which may visually have a negative effect on the unique Danish coasts. A bathing or boat bridge must provide access between land and water and not serve as a living area on the water. The Coastal Directorate therefore has a restrictive practice for furniture on a bridge and boat lifts etc. which are not necessary for the general use of a bridge and which may appear very visible in the coastal landscape. Local conditions are involved in the assessment of the individual case, but local wishes will not always be met. The practice of the Coastal Directorate has been confirmed by the Board of Appeal, and I think that is a little important to emphasise. A change in the division of powers would therefore not in itself constitute a basis for a different administration of the rules, as seems to be the premise of the proposers. However, I see a risk that the proposed transfer of competence could lead to different practices across the country, contrary to the national interests of the state's management of the coastal zone. Municipalities may allow smaller necessary devices, which naturally belong to the bridges to which they can grant permission themselves, e.g.: a handrail for safety, a small kayaking platform or a bath staircase. In conclusion, I can inform you that the government is preparing a change to the notice on bathing and boat bridges, so that the municipalities also take over the power to issue orders to legalise not legally constructed bathing and boat bridges and to allow mooring poles at boat bridges, as mooring poles are considered to be a natural part of the use of a boat bridge and can be placed without dominating the coastal landscape. It is in our common interest that the open coastal landscapes are preserved while at the same time we can bathe, sail, dive, fish and everything else we also want on the Danish coasts. I believe that the current rules help to ensure a balance, which is why the government cannot support the resolution. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20210504184539	Thank you for the floor. Thank you for a good debate today, and thank you to the Minister, and thank all the parties that support this small but fundamental proposal. I would also like to thank the Minister for the fact that the Minister would like to look at the announcement and that we can discuss it further, including in committee. I am listening to the fact that there are some concerns which I hope we will be able to address in the committee's work. This proposal is about simplifying working procedures so that we avoid double administration and unnecessary bureaucracy. It is about creating laws and rules that can be understood by citizens. It is a question of putting decisions close to the citizens. In fact, decisions affecting citizens in the municipalities rather than with the State Coastal Directorate. The proposal isn't about changing the law. The municipalities have already shown that they have competence in the coastal areas, where they are currently responsible for the authorisation of the construction of coastal protection, for the construction of bathing and boat bridges, for injunctions by illegally constructed bathing and boat bridges, and on the way is also the competence to process applications for mooring poles in connection with bathing and boat bridges. We want to bring it all together once and for all, all questions relating to bathing and boat bridges, in one place, so that we avoid unnecessary bureaucracy, so that we have a logical division of tasks and so that ordinary citizens can clearly identify who is in charge of bathing and boat bridges. In Denmark we can boast of very transparent administration, very transparent decision-making processes with laws and rules that can be understood. As legislators, we also want to ensure that decisions are taken close to the citizens, and on many issues we have succeeded in decentralising decisions and moving them out into the municipalities, who know about the local conditions. It is healthy for democracy and good intentions are mostly successful, but not always. This is precisely on the basis of the proposal for a parliamentary decision to amend the regulation on bathing and boatbridges. In concrete terms, it is the case that a swimming pool with a large group of citizens as members or citizens who own a leisure home or a home off the coast is experiencing incomprehensible bureaucracy and regulations when they seek permission to set up a bathing or boat bridge or to change existing bathing and boat bridges. Indeed, there are two authorities involved and the distribution of power between them is very unclear. According to the rules in force, it is therefore the municipality which has the power to grant the permission to establish the ordinary bathing and boat bridges, as we know them off houses and summer houses on the Danish coasts. But then there is the confusion, because who will then deal with any changes that a bath or owner of a bathing or boat bridge wants to make? It is the municipality which administers bathing and boat bridges according to the Order, so it is natural to imagine that it is the municipality that will decide whether a railing, stairway, lift may be there or not, not least because the municipality knows the conditions in the area and has already given permission to the bridge itself. But no, here the bath maker sees that the Coast Directorate goes into the matter and decides that rails or gates must not be there. So we have the small authority, the municipality, which administers and decides on the large layout, namely the bridges themselves, while in several cases it is the large authority, the Coastal Directorate, which administers and decides on the smaller devices on the bridge itself. This is a bit incomprehensible double management. So if the Minister insists that it is reasonable that the competence is shared, that is, that it is the small authority, the municipality, which administers the decision on the large layout, namely the bridge, but the large authority, the Coastal Directorate, which administers and decides on the smaller devices of the bridge itself, we can see that it means unnecessary bureaucracy that two authorities must be required in relation to the division of tasks themselves. It is incomprehensible that smaller structures on and on private bathing bridges should be decided by a state authority when the layout relates to what the bridge is built for. That is why this motion for a resolution has been tabled to amend the regulation on bathing and boatbridges. In this way, municipalities will be given the competence to communicate on the bathing and boat bridges themselves, which are directly related to the purpose of the bathing and boat bridges as a railing, a staircase in the water, a lift to a dinghy or a boat, a sign about low depth or gate. Thus, we place the administration and the authority where it makes sense, that is, close to the citizens, with the municipalities that know the local conditions, thereby making it clear to both authorities and citizens who are doing what, while as legislators we are doing what we can to combat incomprehensible bureaucracy and double administration. I hope that we can find a joint text for a report in committee which envisages making work more flexible, and I hope that we can look into it and change it in the notice. Thank you for the floor and thank you for the debate.

However, the government does not believe that a relocation of tasks will solve the real challenges in this area. There's a risk, popularly said, that the monkey's just moving in. The relocation of tasks may also mean that the efforts are less holistic and combined with other local welfare efforts in the municipalities. It is a fundamental principle of the social area that we, as a society, must meet the social needs of our citizens in close connection with the other needs of our citizens, for example. in relation to employment, schooling, leisure, etc. In addition, a transfer of tasks and a separation of visiting powers and financial responsibility would violate the principle that the municipality that pays the benefits to a citizen under the Services Act should also, as a rule, be the one making the decisions; a principle that contributes to governance in this area, so that we ensure that the total resources in the social field are used in the best possible way to help our most vulnerable citizens. It's a long, cool move to strengthen the specialized social area, it doesn't make any quick snaps or fingertips. New solutions and changes must therefore be thorough and well thought out, and I believe that we can succeed with this. A prerequisite for designing the area wisely is that we have sufficient knowledge. Unfortunately, we know and have known too little about this area for quite many years. That is why the current government, with the support of the Enhedslisten, the Radical Left, the Socialist People's Party and the Alternative, has launched an ambitious and comprehensive evaluation of the specialized social area. The evaluation shall include: identify the services available in the area, the different target groups and their needs, and how the specialised area is structured. The evaluation will also identify and analyse the current distribution of tasks, the financing structure and the financial incentives, the current visitation, the existing specialization mechanisms and quality in the specialized social field. The evaluation must help to provide us with the necessary and thorough knowledge which will enable us to set up the area in order to provide citizens with the best possible help and support to live a good life. Organizations such as: Danske Handicaporganisations and the Social Advisor Association and others have also been out to say that a move in itself does not solve the challenges experienced in the specialized social field. In their eyes, it is about strengthening the search, the level of knowledge, the economy, no matter which authority is responsible. In addition to the formal and major evaluation which we have launched by the government, many others, fortunately, have come up with analyses and ideas for solutions. Because it's about the individual being given the right help. Based on the evaluation, the government will look at what measures can support people with disabilities and vulnerable children and adults in our society. It must be possible to live as normal a life as possible, regardless of residence. But today it is for many a struggle to get the right specialized help. This is mainly due to VKO's municipal reform, which did not take into account the concern about despecialisation. The municipalities are doing the best they can, but they've got a difficult job. We must rectify this, so that in future the individual is offered the right help of the right quality and with the right specialization. ParlaMint-DK_20210504202456	Thank you for that, President. Today is a long-awaited day for citizens with disabilities and their relatives, but especially for the many in #enmillion votes who have tabled the citizens' proposal. Because it moves when speaking with one voice on behalf of so many, and this is imperative, because over time developments have gone the wrong way in the area of disability. In 2007, a bourgeois majority in the Folketing chose to close down the counties and move the handicap area to the municipalities. Many of us were against this, and we were just as many who warned against the despecialisation of the area that would happen: that the built-up knowledge and experience that one had would be tampered with and that the many specialized offers that lay in the counties would disappear. But the Left, the Conservatives and the Danish People's Party were unmovable. Many municipalities were then seen taking offers home and referring citizens to less specialised offers. This is undoubtedly one of the reasons why there are such major problems in the area of disability as there are today. But there is also another important reason, and that is the economy, because there are more people with disabilities today, while the economy of the municipalities has not been followed up at a time when Christiansborg has told the municipalities that it must be made more efficient. Some of those who feel it the most today are the citizens who live with disabilities. Too many people experience that the economy comes before the professional consideration when they apply for help from the municipality. Too many people find that they themselves have to pay for an aid or go down in time at work because they cannot get the help from the municipality. Too many people experience inhumanly long processing times, both in the municipalities and in the Ankestyrelsen. Too many people experience unreasonable demands for documentation such as observation in the private for several days. Too many find themselves deprived of their help without any change in their condition or more. Too many people get neither education nor jobs. The stories are many and heartbreaking, and there are an increasing number of complaints that are flawed, and there is no doubt also a large number of dark ones. There needs to be a reform of the whole area in order to put things right again. We're ready for that in the SF. It must no longer pay for the individual municipality to give the citizen less or no help. In future, it will be professionalism and specialised knowledge that will be the guiding element in the process. This requires more money for the area, and it requires a change in economic incentives. The responsibility must be transferred and linked to the task, not the funding, as described in the #enmillion vote resolution. This also means that we must have specialised knowledge and have built up the capacity we previously had. SF believes that the regions should also have a central role, where centres of knowledge must gather knowledge about the various handicaps and what efforts are needed to enable the individual to live a good and active life. That knowledge is to be used in the search. We want progress packages to be developed over time, where it is the specialist knowledge that sets standards for what you need as a minimum, and it should not be up to the individual municipality alone to assess how much help you need or, in fact, how disabled you are. There are many interpretations of this. These programme packages must, of course, be followed by an individual and holistic plan drawn up in close cooperation with the citizen. There is no easy and quick solution to the problems, but we need to have more major tools to change the unfair situation facing many people with disabilities. Moving the whole area away from the municipalities will not solve it in itself because the regions are not as the counties were. However, it makes very good sense for the regions to have a much more central role and much more tasks in the field of disability. My hope is that a majority will come together now and start work on the reform to ensure proper and dignified conditions for people with disabilities. We owe it to them and their relatives.

ParlaMint-DK_20210504204452	Fundamental changes are needed in the area of disability, because far too many people with disabilities today do not receive the help they need. For Enhedslisten, it is important that we replace the current market management of the area with a special planning that ensures that citizens can get the specialised assistance they need. We want to break the municipalities' visitation monopoly. It may well be that the municipalities must be the public authority that most citizens with disabilities meet first, but when searching for help that requires highly specialised knowledge, we do not believe that the municipalities are able to carry out the task. We want a completely new financing model in which full financial responsibility is removed from the municipalities. We want a fairer and, above all, a more balanced distribution of expenditure, so that we remove the incentive which today is to put the economy ahead of the needs of the citizens and which removes the fact that it is worthwhile for the municipalities today to carry out faulty and erroneous procedures. We want to wait for a final decision on how the organisation, the search, the funding should be in a reform, but we are, on the other hand, impatient to get started with the negotiations to strengthen legal certainty so that it can be in place before we have to negotiate a fundamental reform of the disability area. We have recently presented in the Enhedslisten nine concrete proposals for improving legal certainty, and we will take them with us to the negotiations on such a thing. Thank you once again to the authors of the motion for a resolution, for pushing for change. I am one of those who believe that change will only occur, even in this Chamber, when there is sufficient external pressure, and I hope that we will soon be able to sign up with a fundamental reform of the disability area. We basically want to change the role and tasks of municipalities in relation to the area of disability, and that is why we can support the citizens' proposal.

ParlaMint-DK_20210504210946	Thank you. It's a big day. It's a day I've been looking forward to the day when all parties must confess their colours. I am doing this, firstly, because people with disabilities and their relatives today find that in too many cases municipalities administer illegally, so that they do not get the help they are entitled to. 42 per cent. of the cases which end up in the Ankestyrelsen are either reversed or sent back to the municipality for new treatment. In addition, the processing times are very long, the deadlines are routinely exceeded, and people with disabilities and their relatives tell me again and again almost absurd decisions suggesting a widespread cash-flow thinking in the municipalities. Rasmus, who could not get a waterproof prosthesis so that he could do his job in a catering kitchen, Rasmus, who ended up dying due to numerous failures in the system 18 years old. Lots of kids with challenges that can't get the right school offer. I've seen them in school, depressed, outgoing, inward-looking, without hope and without the fantastic spark of life and joy that should be a natural part of every child's life. Today, as a society, we are abandoning all those people with disabilities who are not being helped, who are struggling bravely, but who are facing the wall of box thinking and counter poaching out in the municipalities. The disabled area came under the municipalities in 2007 in connection with the municipal reform. It was a mistake. Municipalities are not capable of the task, and people with disabilities and their families become losers. As a society, we cannot accept that. That's where the system needs to be broken down, and something new needs to be built up. I and New Citizens will give all people with disabilities a uniform treatment. The conditions governing support and assistance must be the same for each person with disabilities, whether you live in Gedser or in Skagen. And if you move across a municipality border, you have to be able to take your help with you and not start again by applying again in a new municipality. To compensate for the laxity and maladministration of the municipalities, we will set up a national professional council which will be responsible for providing assistance and services to people with disabilities. Benefits and aid are financed by the State and are therefore taken away from the municipal cash-flow thinking. The Council establishes itself with teams across the country so that there is never far to the nearest. Under the National Professional Council, we will employ teams of professionals to meet and assess the individual with disabilities as a whole person. All aspects of the person's life situation must be taken into account in relation to relatives, friends, housing situation, disability, jobs, education, etc., and the same team follows the individual as far as possible, provides the necessary assistance and regularly follows up to adapt the aid as needed. It must be the end of a sea of municipal case officers where one hand does not know what the other does. Each team should be self-organising and autonomous under the responsibility of the law and coordination of the national council. Now the politicians will say that it will be more expensive. Yes, in isolation, a new system based on a stronger professionalism in the case-file will become more expensive, because fewer changes in the appeal board and the removal of the municipalities' ability to speculate on giving people with disabilities less than they are entitled to, everything else will just lead to more people getting more faster. But there is also another side of the coin that is important to get with where simpler rules, correct and faster decisions will help the individual with disabilities to speed up clarification and, in many cases, to a greater degree of self-sufficiency. The family's financial loss in terms of lost labour income, when the municipality takes long-term offers of help, where the relatives have to care for family members with disabilities themselves for long periods of time, also causes a socio-economic loss and reduces income from taxes and charges, so overall I am not actually convinced that it will be more expensive for society to create a better system to provide just the right help for people with disabilities. It can actually end up with a pure win-win situation where both the individual with disabilities, the relatives and the rest of society get something good out of it. Of course, new citizens support this citizens' proposal, which has been brought to the Parliament hall by the committed movement #enmillion votes. It is time for the handicap area to come out of the city's claws; it is urgent. Thank you for the floor.

ParlaMint-DK_20210504220648	Thank you very much, President. The motion for a resolution proposes abolishing the taxation of employer-paid telephones and the Internet, as has been advocated. There's not much new in that. The former VLAK government, together with the Danish People's Party, adopted such a tax exemption which did not come into force. It was a skewed tax relief that this government could not support, and I shall come back to that. Let me briefly summarise the rules in force. According to the rules they are today, private availability is taxed on employer-paid free telephone and internet with an amount of approx. 2021. 3,000 kroner. The amount covers both the telephone system, if you like, the establishment of the Internet connection, consumption and subscription. I would, of course, like to stress that this is not a matter of taxation on the use of a working tool here. It is, so to speak, employer-paid telephone and Internet that are at the private disposal of the individual who is taxed. The current rules therefore take account of the fact that there should be no taxation of telephones and the Internet, which is used exclusively in terms of work. If e.g. a mobile phone is used exclusively in terms of work, there will be no taxation or tax if it is taken home because it is necessary for the performance of the work • provided that an agreement has been reached that the phone is not to be used privately and the employer controls it. An Internet connection connected to a worker's computer is also not taxed when the computer has access to the workplace network. We made it very clear in the context of the 2020 Finance Act that this government will reverse the biased tax cuts of the previous government, which have therefore been given to wealthy groups in our society. We did so together with the Radical Left, the SF, the Enhedslisten and the Alternative among others. By maintaining taxation on what we are discussing here, i.e. among other things. Free phone. The tax exemption for employer-paid telephones and the Internet, which was adopted by the votes of the Left, the Conservative People's Party, the Liberal Alliance and the Danish People's Party, mainly benefited those with the highest incomes. Thus, more than half of top tax payers are estimated to have free phones. On the other hand, only approx. 6% by non-top tax payers free phone. So you can probably call this awkward. By maintaining taxation, we took a concrete step towards reducing income disparities in our society and, at the same time, we could maintain the principle that wages should be taxed equally, regardless of whether they are paid in cash or in employee benefits, such as VAT. Free phone. So once again it cannot come as a surprise that the government cannot support this motion for a resolution.

ParlaMint-DK_20210504230308	Here is another proposal from the Conservative People's Party, this time to raise the top tax limit and increase the employment allowance. But I would like to try to address another problem, because I actually think it is a major problem that the income tax system has simply become too complicated. I think there are very few Danes out there who can actually sit down and calculate their own taxes or find out whether it is worth taking an extra shift at all. We just got the annual statement so that people could see if they had to pay taxes or if they had to have money back, and I actually think that by far most people took it a bit like a lottery. Maybe you got 5,000 kroner. back, but why it was 5,000 kroner, you don't know, or why you had to pay 2,000 kroner, you don't know either, because no one can figure it out. In fact, I think it is an independent problem, which is actually almost greater than whether we should set taxes up or down. We should first focus on making it simpler, because that is a much bigger problem. There are labour market contributions, there are job deductions, there are employment deductions, there is bottom tax, there is municipal tax, there is top tax, there is something called biased tax ceiling, according to what the municipal tax is, there is equational deduction, there is church tax, and if you are married then you are jointly taxed with a spouse, and then there are some deductions that you can share with the other, and there are other deductions that you cannot share. If you are divorced, you have to consider whether there is child support that you can then deduct from taxes and other things. It's to tell you the truth, it's a jungle. Frankly, I think that if it was worthwhile to work and if you have the dedication that is quite real when you are asked whether you can take an extra shift on Saturday at the slaughterhouse or at the dairy, or where you now work, I do not think anyone could see through what they actually have to pay in tax for that Saturday, and whether it can pay for them, and I do not really think you can be familiar with that. Should it not be an independent point that you should actually be able to understand why you pay the tax you pay? Then you can be mad that it is too high or that it is too low, but at least I think it is an important agenda to make it simpler before we start to turn the rates up and down, so that we try to do it in a way that you can actually see through. That was a bit of a setback in relation to the specific proposal. If I jump back to it, I have suffered the same challenge as I have faced with the others, because the proposal does not, in fact, show how much it costs. Right here, I've actually looked it up myself, and I understand that the top tax costs 1.2 billion. kr., and that the employment allowance costs 0.7 billion. kr., that is somewhere between 1.9 billion. and 2 billion. kr. Nor does it appear from the proposal where the money is to come from, and that makes it a little difficult. It refers to labour market reforms, but what labour market reforms? That would be nice to know. So we end up in the same impasse as we do with the other proposals, namely that it is difficult to deal with a proposal with which there is neither price nor funding. We end up voting no, also for the reason that we would rather focus on making it simpler so that people can actually see through whether working is worthwhile before changing the rates that exist today.

Denmark has the OECD's biggest tax burden, is the gloomy announcement, but it is actually a bad target for the real tax burden. Firstly, the Danish tax burden is increased by grossing up social transfers, i.e. paying them out and then deducting the tax. Secondly, we pay direct transfers, for example. children's and young people's benefits. Instead, if it were made a deduction, it would also reduce the tax burden. A number of subsidies could also be converted into deductions, and that would also reduce the tax burden. This could also be done without affecting the economy of households, but it would lower the tax burden to below 40%. Secondly, the tax burden is also affected by cyclical developments. If e.g. tax revenue is increasing relatively more than gross domestic product, and then the tax burden is increasing, and it can actually do so even if tax reductions are made.
